# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title pa19 Model

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}
class CellStack {
    count : int
    stack : List<FillableCell>
    getUndoCount() : int
    pop() : FillableCell
    push(cell : FillableCell) : void
}
class Coordinate {
    col : int
    row : int
    add(other : Coordinate) : Coordinate
    equals(obj : EJavaObject) : boolean
}
class DelayBar {
    currentValue : int
    initialValue : int
    countdown() : void
    distance() : int
}
class FillableCell {
    pipe : Pipe
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Game {
    numOfSteps : int
    cellStack : CellStack
    delayBar : DelayBar
    map : Map
    pipeQueue : PipeQueue
    hasLost() : boolean
    hasWon() : boolean
    placePipe(row : int, col : char) : boolean
    skipPipe() : void
    undoStep() : boolean
    updateState() : void
}
class Map {
    cell : cellarray
    cols : int
    prevFilledDistance : EIntegerObject
    prevFilledTiles : int
    rows : int
    filledTiles : List<Coordinate>
    sinkCell : TerminationCell
    sourceCell : TerminationCell
    checkPath() : boolean
    fillBeginTile() : void
    fillTiles(distance : int) : void
    hasLost() : boolean
    tryPlacePipe(row : int, col : int, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    tryPlacePipe1(coord : Coordinate, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    undo(coord : Coordinate) : void
}
abstract class MapElement {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Pa19Helper {
    cellFromChar(c : char, coord : Coordinate, terminationType : TerminationType) : Cell
    createFillableCell(coord : Coordinate, pipes : Pipe) : FillableCell
    createGame(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cells : cellarray, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    createMap(rows : int, cols : int, cells : cellarray) : Map
    createTerminationCell(coord : Coordinate, type : TerminationType, dir : Direction) : TerminationCell
    createWall(coord : Coordinate) : Wall
    gameFromString(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cellsRep : String, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    getOffset(dir : Direction) : Coordinate
    getOpposite(dir : Direction) : Direction
    mapFromString(rows : int, cols : int, cellsRep : String) : Map
    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe
}
class Pipe {
    filled : boolean
    shape : PipeShape
    getConnections() : List<Direction>
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class PipeQueue {
    maxGenLength : int
    pipeQueue : List<Pipe>
    consume() : void
    generateNewPipe() : Pipe
    peek() : Pipe
    undo(pipe : Pipe) : void
}
class TerminationCell {
    filled : boolean
    pointingTo : Direction
    type : TerminationType
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Wall {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
enum Direction {
    UP
    DOWN
    LEFT
    RIGHT
}
enum PipeShape {
    HORIZONTAL
    VERTICAL
    TOP_LEFT
    TOP_RIGHT
    BOTTOM_LEFT
    BOTTOM_RIGHT
    CROSS
}
enum TerminationType {
    SOURCE
    SINK
}

Cell --|> MapElement
Cell *-- Coordinate : coord
CellStack *-- "*" FillableCell : stack
FillableCell --|> Cell
FillableCell *-- Pipe : pipe
Game *-- CellStack : cellStack
Game *-- DelayBar : delayBar
Game *-- Map : map
Game *-- PipeQueue : pipeQueue
Map *-- "*" Coordinate : filledTiles
Map --> TerminationCell : sinkCell
Map --> TerminationCell : sourceCell
Pipe --|> MapElement
PipeQueue *-- "*" Pipe : pipeQueue
TerminationCell --|> Cell
Wall --|> Cell
@enduml
```

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

```plantuml
title pa19 Model

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}
class CellStack {
    count : int
    stack : List<FillableCell>

    col : int
    row : int

    equals(obj : EJavaObject) : boolean

    currentValue : int
    initialValue : int
    countdown() : void
    distance() : int
}

    pipe : Pipe
    toSingleChar() : char
}

    numOfSteps : int
    cellStack : CellStack
    delayBar : DelayBar
    map : Map
    pipeQueue : PipeQueue

    hasWon() : boolean
    placePipe(row : int, col : char) : boolean
    skipPipe() : void
    undoStep() : boolean
    updateState() : void
}

    cell : cellarray
    cols : int
    prevFilledDistance : EIntegerObject
    prevFilledTiles : int

    sourceCell : TerminationCell

    tryPlacePipe(row : int, col : int, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    tryPlacePipe1(coord : Coordinate, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    undo(coord : Coordinate) : void
}

    toSingleChar() : char
}

    cellFromChar(c : char, coord : Coordinate, terminationType : TerminationType) : Cell
    createFillableCell(coord : Coordinate, pipes : Pipe) : FillableCell
    createGame(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cells : cellarray, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    createMap(rows : int, cols : int, cells : cellarray) : Map
    createTerminationCell(coord : Coordinate, type : TerminationType, dir : Direction) : TerminationCell
    createWall(coord : Coordinate) : Wall
    gameFromString(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cellsRep : String, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    getOffset(dir : Direction) : Coordinate
    getOpposite(dir : Direction) : Direction
    mapFromString(rows : int, cols : int, cellsRep : String) : Map
    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe
}
class Pipe {
    filled : boolean
    shape : PipeShape
    getConnections() : List<Direction>
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class PipeQueue {
    maxGenLength : int
    pipeQueue : List<Pipe>
    consume() : void
    generateNewPipe() : Pipe
    peek() : Pipe
    undo(pipe : Pipe) : void
}

    filled : boolean
    pointingTo : Direction
    type : TerminationType

    toSingleChar() : char
}

    UP
    DOWN
    LEFT
    RIGHT

    HORIZONTAL
    VERTICAL
    TOP_LEFT
    TOP_RIGHT
    BOTTOM_LEFT
    BOTTOM_RIGHT
    CROSS
}

    SOURCE
    SINK
}
```

# Operation Specifications
Game::skipPipe
Summary: Discards the current head pipe from the queue without placing it and advances the step counter.
Algorithm: Invoke pipeQueue.consume(). Increment numOfSteps by 1.
Related Features: numOfSteps, pipeQueue
Related Classes: PipeQueue
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty.
Post-condition: Head pipe removed from queue, step count incremented by 1.

Game::hasWon
Summary: Checks if a valid continuous pipe path exists from the SOURCE to the SINK.
Algorithm: Delegate to map.checkPath() and return the boolean result.
Related Features: map
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if SOURCE connects to SINK via pipes, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Game instance is active.
Post-condition: No side effects; purely evaluates connectivity.

Game::hasLost
Summary: Determines if the player has lost the game due to water stagnation.
Algorithm: Delegate to map.hasLost() and return the boolean result.
Related Features: map
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if loss condition is met, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Game instance is active and map state is current.
Post-condition: No side effects; purely evaluates game state.

Game::undoStep
Summary: Reverts the most recent successful placement action, restoring the pipe to the queue and clearing the map.
Algorithm: If cellStack is empty, return false. Pop cell from cellStack. Retrieve cell's coordinate. Call map.undo(coord). Call pipeQueue.undo(cell.pipe). Increment numOfSteps. Return true.
Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
Related Classes: CellStack, PipeQueue, Map, Coordinate, Pipe
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if undo succeeded, false if no steps available to undo.
Pre-condition: At least one placement step exists in the stack.
Post-condition: Specified cell cleared on map, original pipe restored to queue head, step count incremented.

Game::placePipe
Summary: Places the head pipe from the queue onto the map at a player-specified coordinate.
Algorithm: Convert column char to internal index: colIndex = col - 'A' + 1. Retrieve pipe via pipeQueue.consume(). Attempt placement via map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe). If successful, push the resulting cell to cellStack and increment numOfSteps. Return true on success, false on failure.
Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack, Pipe, Coordinate
Input: row : int - player-facing row index (directly maps to internal array row); col : char - player-facing column letter (e.g., 'A' maps to internal index 1).
Output: boolean - true if pipe placed successfully, false if placement failed or queue was empty.
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty; row is within valid range; col is between 'A' and 'Z'.
Post-condition: Head pipe consumed from queue, placed on map if valid, cell pushed to stack, step count incremented.

Game::updateState
Summary: Advances the simulation by one time step: processes delay countdown and propagates water flow.
Algorithm: Call delayBar.countdown(). Compute distance = delayBar.distance(). If distance > 0, call map.fillTiles(distance). Update map's tracking variables for the next round.
Related Features: delayBar, map
Related Classes: DelayBar, Map
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: Game loop is running; map and delay bar are initialized.
Post-condition: Countdown decremented; water propagates up to the calculated distance if delay has expired; map state updated.

# Output Format
Put the revised specifications in a single block as illustrated below:

```result

class_1::operation_1
/*
 * revised description for operation_1
 */

class_1::operation_2
/*
 * revised description for this operation_2
 */

...
```

15:48:36.615 [main] DEBUG org.springframework.web.client.DefaultRestClient -- Writing [ChatCompletionRequest[messages=[ChatCompletionMessage[rawContent=# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

Related Features: numOfSteps, pipeQueue
Related Classes: PipeQueue
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty.
Post-condition: Head pipe removed from queue, step count incremented by 1.

Game::hasWon
Summary: Checks if a valid continuous pipe path exists from the SOURCE to the SINK.
Algorithm: Delegate to map.checkPath() and return the boolean result.
Related Features: map
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if SOURCE connects to SINK via pipes, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Game instance is active.
Post-condition: No side effects; purely evaluates connectivity.

Game::hasLost
Summary: Determines if the player has lost the game due to water stagnation.
Algorithm: Delegate to map.hasLost() and return the boolean result.
Related Features: map
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if loss condition is met, false otherwise.

Post-condition: No side effects; purely evaluates game state.

Game::undoStep
Summary: Reverts the most recent successful placement action, restoring the pipe to the queue and clearing the map.
Algorithm: If cellStack is empty, return false. Pop cell from cellStack. Retrieve cell's coordinate. Call map.undo(coord). Call pipeQueue.undo(cell.pipe). Increment numOfSteps. Return true.

Post-condition: Specified cell cleared on map, original pipe restored to queue head, step count incremented.

Game::placePipe
Summary: Places the head pipe from the queue onto the map at a player-specified coordinate.
Algorithm: Convert column char to internal index: colIndex = col - 'A' + 1. Retrieve pipe via pipeQueue.consume(). Attempt placement via map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe). If successful, push the resulting cell to cellStack and increment numOfSteps. Return true on success, false on failure.
Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack, Pipe, Coordinate
Input: row : int - player-facing row index (directly maps to internal array row); col : char - player-facing column letter (e.g., 'A' maps to internal index 1).
Output: boolean - true if pipe placed successfully, false if placement failed or queue was empty.
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty; row is within valid range; col is between 'A' and 'Z'.
Post-condition: Head pipe consumed from queue, placed on map if valid, cell pushed to stack, step count incremented.

Game::updateState
Summary: Advances the simulation by one time step: processes delay countdown and propagates water flow.
Algorithm: Call delayBar.countdown(). Compute distance = delayBar.distance(). If distance > 0, call map.fillTiles(distance). Update map's tracking variables for the next round.
Related Features: delayBar, map
Related Classes: DelayBar, Map
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: Game loop is running; map and delay bar are initialized.
Post-condition: Countdown decremented; water propagates up to the calculated distance if delay has expired; map state updated.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Operation Specifications
Game::skipPipe
Summary: Discards the current head pipe from the queue without placing it and advances the step counter.
Algorithm: 1. Invoke pipeQueue.consume() to remove the head pipe. 2. Increment numOfSteps by 1.
Related Features: numOfSteps, pipeQueue
Related Classes: PipeQueue
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty.
Post-condition: Head pipe is removed from the queue, numOfSteps is incremented by 1.

Game::hasWon
Summary: Checks if a valid continuous pipe path exists from the SOURCE to the SINK.
Algorithm: Delegate to map.checkPath() and return the boolean result. checkPath() performs a BFS traversal starting from the SOURCE's pointingTo direction, following pipe connection directions, and returns true if the SINK cell is reached.
Related Features: map
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Game instance is active; map contains valid SOURCE and SINK cells.
Post-condition: No side effects; purely evaluates connectivity state.

Game::hasLost
Summary: Determines if the player has lost the game due to water stagnation.
Algorithm: 1. Check if delayBar.distance() > 0 (delay has expired). 2. If true, return map.getPrevFilledTiles() == 0. Otherwise, return false. Loss is only evaluated after the countdown completes and no new tiles were filled in the previous round.
Related Features: delayBar, map
Related Classes: DelayBar, Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if loss condition is met, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Game instance is active and map/delay bar states are current.
Post-condition: No side effects; purely evaluates loss state based on delay completion and previous fill progress.

Game::undoStep
Summary: Reverts the most recent successful placement action, restoring the pipe to the queue and clearing the map.
Algorithm: 1. If cellStack is empty, return false. 2. Pop the top FillableCell from cellStack. 3. Retrieve the coordinate from the cell. 4. Call map.undo(coord) to clear the cell on the map. 5. Call pipeQueue.undo(cell.getPipe()) to restore the pipe to the queue head. 6. Increment numOfSteps by 1. 7. Return true.
Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
Related Classes: CellStack, PipeQueue, Map, Coordinate, Pipe
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if undo succeeded, false if no steps available to undo.
Pre-condition: At least one placement step exists in the cellStack.
Post-condition: Specified cell cleared on map, original pipe restored to queue head, numOfSteps incremented by 1.

Game::placePipe
Summary: Places the head pipe from the queue onto the map at a player-specified coordinate.
Algorithm: 1. Convert column character to internal index: colIndex = col - 'A' + 1. 2. Retrieve the head pipe from pipeQueue (via peek()/consume()). 3. Call map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe). 4. If successful, push the placed FillableCell to cellStack and increment numOfSteps by 1. 5. Return true on success, false on failure.
Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack, Pipe, Coordinate
Input: row : int - player-facing row index (directly maps to internal array row); col : char - player-facing column letter (e.g., 'A' maps to internal index 1).
Output: boolean - true if pipe placed successfully, false if placement failed or queue was empty.
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty; row and col correspond to valid playable area indices [1..rows-2].
Post-condition: Head pipe consumed from queue, placed on map if valid, corresponding FillableCell pushed to stack, numOfSteps incremented.

Game::updateState
Summary: Advances the simulation by one time step: processes delay countdown and propagates water flow.
Algorithm: 1. Call delayBar.countdown() to decrement the timer. 2. Calculate distance = delayBar.distance(). 3. If distance > 0, call map.fillTiles(distance) to propagate water using BFS-based expansion along matching pipe connections. The fillTiles method internally handles marking filled pipes and updating tracking variables (filledTiles, prevFilledTiles).
Related Features: delayBar, map
Related Classes: DelayBar, Map
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: Game loop is running; delayBar and map are initialized; fillBeginTile() has been called initially.
Post-condition: Countdown decremented; if delay expired, water propagates up to the calculated distance; map's internal fill state and tracking variables updated accordingly.

```text
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Operation Specifications
Game::skipPipe
Summary: Discards the current head pipe from the queue without placing it and advances the step counter.
Algorithm: 1. Invoke pipeQueue.consume() to remove the head pipe. 2. Increment numOfSteps by 1.
Related Features: numOfSteps, pipeQueue
Related Classes: PipeQueue
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty.
Post-condition: Head pipe is removed from the queue, numOfSteps is incremented by 1.

Game::hasWon
Summary: Checks if a valid continuous pipe path exists from the SOURCE to the SINK.
Algorithm: Delegate to map.checkPath() and return the boolean result. checkPath() performs a BFS traversal starting from the SOURCE's pointingTo direction, following pipe connection directions, and returns true if the SINK cell is reached.
Related Features: map
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Game instance is active; map contains valid SOURCE and SINK cells.
Post-condition: No side effects; purely evaluates connectivity state.

Game::hasLost
Summary: Determines if the player has lost the game due to water stagnation.
Algorithm: 1. Check if delayBar.distance() > 0 (delay has expired). 2. If true, return map.getPrevFilledTiles() == 0. Otherwise, return false. Loss is only evaluated after the countdown completes and no new tiles were filled in the previous round.
Related Features: delayBar, map
Related Classes: DelayBar, Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if loss condition is met, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The Game instance is active and map/delay bar states are current.
Post-condition: No side effects; purely evaluates loss state based on delay completion and previous fill progress.

Game::undoStep
Summary: Reverts the most recent successful placement action, restoring the pipe to the queue and clearing the map.
Algorithm: 1. If cellStack is empty, return false. 2. Pop the top FillableCell from cellStack. 3. Retrieve the coordinate from the cell. 4. Call map.undo(coord) to clear the cell on the map. 5. Call pipeQueue.undo(cell.getPipe()) to restore the pipe to the queue head. 6. Increment numOfSteps by 1. 7. Return true.
Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
Related Classes: CellStack, PipeQueue, Map, Coordinate, Pipe
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if undo succeeded, false if no steps available to undo.
Pre-condition: At least one placement step exists in the cellStack.
Post-condition: Specified cell cleared on map, original pipe restored to queue head, numOfSteps incremented by 1.

Game::placePipe
Summary: Places the head pipe from the queue onto the map at a player-specified coordinate.
Algorithm: 1. Convert column character to internal index: colIndex = col - 'A' + 1. 2. Retrieve the head pipe from pipeQueue (via peek()/consume()). 3. Call map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe). 4. If successful, push the placed FillableCell to cellStack and increment numOfSteps by 1. 5. Return true on success, false on failure.
Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack, Pipe, Coordinate
Input: row : int - player-facing row index (directly maps to internal array row); col : char - player-facing column letter (e.g., 'A' maps to internal index 1).
Output: boolean - true if pipe placed successfully, false if placement failed or queue was empty.
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty; row and col correspond to valid playable area indices [1..rows-2].
Post-condition: Head pipe consumed from queue, placed on map if valid, corresponding FillableCell pushed to stack, numOfSteps incremented.

Game::updateState
Summary: Advances the simulation by one time step: processes delay countdown and propagates water flow.
Algorithm: 1. Call delayBar.countdown() to decrement the timer. 2. Calculate distance = delayBar.distance(). 3. If distance > 0, call map.fillTiles(distance) to propagate water using BFS-based expansion along matching pipe connections. The fillTiles method internally handles marking filled pipes and updating tracking variables (filledTiles, prevFilledTiles).
Related Features: delayBar, map
Related Classes: DelayBar, Map
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: Game loop is running; delayBar and map are initialized; fillBeginTile() has been called initially.
Post-condition: Countdown decremented; if delay expired, water propagates up to the calculated distance; map's internal fill state and tracking variables updated accordingly.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}
class CellStack {
    count : int
    stack : List<FillableCell>
    getUndoCount() : int
    pop() : FillableCell
    push(cell : FillableCell) : void
}

    col : int
    row : int
    add(other : Coordinate) : Coordinate
    equals(obj : EJavaObject) : boolean
}

    currentValue : int
    initialValue : int
    countdown() : void
    distance() : int
}

    pipe : Pipe
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Game {
    numOfSteps : int
    cellStack : CellStack
    delayBar : DelayBar
    map : Map
    pipeQueue : PipeQueue
    hasLost() : boolean
    hasWon() : boolean
    placePipe(row : int, col : char) : boolean

    cols : int

    prevFilledTiles : int
    rows : int
    filledTiles : List<Coordinate>
    sinkCell : TerminationCell
    sourceCell : TerminationCell
    checkPath() : boolean
    fillBeginTile() : void
    fillTiles(distance : int) : void
    hasLost() : boolean
    tryPlacePipe(row : int, col : int, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    tryPlacePipe1(coord : Coordinate, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    undo(coord : Coordinate) : void
}
abstract class MapElement {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Pa19Helper {
    cellFromChar(c : char, coord : Coordinate, terminationType : TerminationType) : Cell
    createFillableCell(coord : Coordinate, pipes : Pipe) : FillableCell
    createGame(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cells : cellarray, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game

    createTerminationCell(coord : Coordinate, type : TerminationType, dir : Direction) : TerminationCell

    getOpposite(dir : Direction) : Direction
    mapFromString(rows : int, cols : int, cellsRep : String) : Map
    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe
```

# Operation Specifications
CellStack::getUndoCount
Summary: Returns the total number of successful undo operations performed since initialization.
Algorithm: Return the value stored in the 'count' field.
Related Features: count
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: int - the cumulative count of successful undo actions.
Pre-condition: The CellStack object is properly initialized.
Post-condition: The internal state remains unchanged; only the counter value is retrieved.

CellStack::pop
Summary: Removes the most recently placed cell from the top of the stack and returns it. Marks the pop as successful.
Algorithm: If stack is empty, throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. Otherwise, retrieve the last element from 'stack', remove it, increment 'count' by 1, and return the removed element.
Related Features: stack, count
Related Classes: FillableCell
Input: None
Output: FillableCell - the cell that was at the top of the stack before removal.
Pre-condition: The stack is not empty.
Post-condition: The top cell is removed from 'stack'; 'count' is incremented by 1.

CellStack::push
Summary: Adds a placed cell to the top of the stack to track placement history.
Algorithm: Append the provided 'cell' to the end of the 'stack' list.
Related Features: stack
Related Classes: FillableCell
Input: cell : FillableCell - the cell instance that was just placed on the game map.
Output: void
Pre-condition: 'cell' is not null.
Post-condition: 'cell' is appended to 'stack', making it the new top element.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
