# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

```text
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation.
```

# Operation Specifications
Map::hasLost
Summary: Evaluates if water flow has stalled completely without progress.
Algorithm: Return prevFilledTiles == 0.
Related Features: prevFilledTiles
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if zero new tiles were filled in the previous propagation round, indicating stagnation.
Pre-condition: Map state reflects current propagation results.
Post-condition: No state modification; returns stagnation status.

Map::tryPlacePipe
Summary: Validates boundaries and cell eligibility, then assigns a pipe to a playable cell.
Algorithm: Check if row and col are within [1..rows-2]. Retrieve cell at cells[row][col]. Verify cell is instance of FillableCell and currently unoccupied (pipe == null). If all pass, set cell.pipe = pipe and return true. Otherwise return false.
Related Features: cell (2D array), rows, cols
Related Classes: FillableCell, Pipe, Coordinate
Input: row : int, col : int - internal array indices aligned with playable area (no offset conversion needed); pipe : Pipe - the pipe shape to place.
Output: boolean - true if placement succeeded, false if out of bounds, wrong cell type, or already occupied.
Pre-condition: Coordinates fall within the playable grid area.
Post-condition: Target cell assigned the pipe if valid; otherwise, map remains unchanged.

Map::undo
Summary: Clears a previously placed pipe from a specific map coordinate.
Algorithm: Access cell at coord.row and coord.col. Set cell.pipe = null. Optionally remove coord from filledTiles if it was part of the water network.
Related Features: cell, filledTiles
Related Classes: Coordinate, FillableCell
Input: coord : Coordinate - the internal coordinates of the cell to clear.
Output: void
Pre-condition: The cell at the given coordinate exists and contains a placed pipe.
Post-condition: Pipe reference removed from the cell; cell reverts to empty state.

Map::fillTiles
Summary: Propagates water across connected pipes up to a specified distance using Breadth-First Search (BFS).
Algorithm: Initialize BFS queue with existing filledTiles. For each iteration up to 'distance': dequeue tile, iterate through its connection directions. For each neighbor, check if it's a valid pipe/termination and if the connecting direction is opposite to the current tile's connection. If valid and not yet filled, mark filled, add to filledTiles, and enqueue. Track how many tiles were filled this round.
Related Features: filledTiles, sourceCell, sinkCell, prevFilledTiles
Related Classes: Coordinate, Direction, Pipe, TerminationCell
Input: distance : int - maximum number of steps water can propagate this round.
Output: void
Pre-condition: sourceCell is already filled via fillBeginTile(); distance >= 0.
Post-condition: New reachable tiles within 'distance' are marked filled; filledTiles list updated; prevFilledTiles records count of newly filled tiles.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:

3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Operation Specifications
Pipe::getConnections
Summary: Returns the cardinal directions in which this pipe connects to adjacent cells.
Algorithm: Switch on shape. Return corresponding Direction[] mapped to HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, etc. If shape is unrecognized, throw IllegalStateException("Unknown shape").
Related Features: shape
Related Classes: Direction, PipeShape
Input: None
Output: List<Direction> - array/list of connection directions.
Pre-condition: The pipe's shape is properly initialized.
Post-condition: Returns accurate connection vectors; throws exception for invalid state.

```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

```plantuml
title pa19 Model

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}

}

    currentValue : int

    distance() : int
}

    pipe : Pipe
    toSingleChar() : char
}

    numOfSteps : int
    cellStack : CellStack
    delayBar : DelayBar
    map : Map

    hasWon() : boolean

    cols : int

    toSingleChar() : char
}

    getOffset(dir : Direction) : Coordinate
    getOpposite(dir : Direction) : Direction
    mapFromString(rows : int, cols : int, cellsRep : String) : Map
    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe
}

    filled : boolean
    shape : PipeShape
    getConnections() : List<Direction>
    toSingleChar() : char
}

    maxGenLength : int

    filled : boolean
    pointingTo : Direction

    BOTTOM_RIGHT

    SINK
```

# Operation Specifications
DelayBar::distance
Summary: Calculates the current water propagation distance based on the countdown state.
Algorithm: Return -currentValue.
Related Features: currentValue
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: int - negative integer during countdown phase, zero at transition, positive integer indicating flow distance after delay ends.
Pre-condition: The DelayBar object is initialized.
Post-condition: No state modification; returns the computed distance value.

DelayBar::countdown
Summary: Decrements the internal timer by 1 to simulate the passage of a game round.
Algorithm: currentValue = currentValue - 1.
Related Features: currentValue
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: The DelayBar object is initialized.
Post-condition: 'currentValue' is reduced by exactly 1.

4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

```plantuml
title pa19 Model

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}

    toSingleChar() : char

    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe

    toSingleChar() : char

    SINK
```

```text
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Operation Specifications
PipeQueue::peek
Summary: Inspects the next pipe to be used without removing it from the queue.
Algorithm: Return pipeQueue.get(0).
Related Features: pipeQueue
Related Classes: Pipe
Input: None
Output: Pipe - the head pipe of the queue.
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty.
Post-condition: No state modification; returns reference to head pipe.

PipeQueue::generateNewPipe
Summary: Creates a new pipe with a randomly selected shape and appends it to the queue.
Algorithm: Select a random enum value from PipeShape. Instantiate a new Pipe with the selected shape. Add the pipe to the end of pipeQueue. Return the new pipe.
Related Features: pipeQueue, maxGenLength
Related Classes: Pipe, PipeShape
Input: None
Output: Pipe - the newly created pipe instance.
Pre-condition: Queue has space below maxGenLength (typically called internally by consume).
Post-condition: A new random pipe is appended to the tail of the queue.

PipeQueue::consume
Summary: Removes the head pipe from the queue and automatically refills the queue to its maximum capacity.
Algorithm: Remove the first element from pipeQueue. While pipeQueue.size() < maxGenLength, call generateNewPipe() and add the result to the end of pipeQueue.
Related Features: pipeQueue, maxGenLength
Related Classes: Pipe
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty.
Post-condition: Original head pipe discarded; queue size restored to maxGenLength with newly generated pipes.

PipeQueue::undo
Summary: Restores a discarded pipe back to the front of the queue for immediate reuse.
Algorithm: Insert the provided 'pipe' at index 0 of pipeQueue. Shift existing elements right.
Related Features: pipeQueue
Related Classes: Pipe
Input: pipe : Pipe - the pipe instance to restore to the head position.
Output: void
Pre-condition: Pipe is a valid instance.
Post-condition: Provided pipe becomes the new head of the queue.

```text
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.

  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.
```

Algorithm: Remove the first element from pipeQueue. While pipeQueue.size() < maxGenLength, invoke generateNewPipe() to append newly generated pipes until capacity is restored.
Related Features: pipeQueue, maxGenLength

Output: void

Algorithm: Insert the provided pipe argument at index 0 of pipeQueue. Existing elements are shifted to higher indices.
Related Features: pipeQueue
Related Classes: Pipe
Input: pipe : Pipe - the pipe instance to restore to the head position.
Output: void

   * Summary: Places the head pipe from the queue onto the map at a player-specified coordinate.
   * Algorithm: 1. Convert column character to internal index: colIndex = col - 'A' + 1. 2. Retrieve the head pipe from pipeQueue (via peek()/consume()). 3. Call map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe). 4. If successful, push the placed FillableCell to cellStack and increment numOfSteps by 1. 5. Return true on success, false on failure.
   * Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
   * Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack, Pipe
   * Input: row : int - player-facing row index (directly maps to internal array row); col : char - player-facing column letter (e.g., 'A' maps to internal index 1).
   * Output: boolean - true if pipe placed successfully, false if placement failed or queue was empty.
   * Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty; row is in [1..rows-2] and col character maps to a valid column index in [1..cols-2].
   * Post-condition: Head pipe consumed from queue, placed on map if valid, corresponding FillableCell pushed to stack, numOfSteps incremented.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean placePipe(final int row, final char col)
  {
    int colIndex = col - 'A' + 1;
    Pipe pipe = getPipeQueue().peek();
    if (pipe == null) {
        return false;
    }
    getPipeQueue().consume();
    FillableCell cell = getMap().tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe);
    if (cell != null) {
        getCellStack().push(cell);
        setNumOfSteps(getNumOfSteps() + 1);
        return true;
    }
    return false;
  }

  /**

   * <!-- end-user-doc -->

```java
   * Algorithm: Append the provided 'cell' to the end of the 'stack' list.
   * Related Features: stack
   * Related Classes: FillableCell
   * Input: cell : FillableCell - the cell instance that was just placed on the game map.
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: 'cell' is not null.
   * Post-condition: 'cell' is appended to 'stack', making it the new top element.
```

   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void push(final FillableCell cell)
  {
    getStack().add(cell);
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Removes the most recently placed cell from the top of the stack and returns it. Marks the pop as successful.
   * Algorithm: If stack is empty, throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. Otherwise, retrieve the last element from 'stack', remove it, increment 'count' by 1, and return the removed element.
   * Related Features: stack, count
   * Related Classes: FillableCell
   * Input: None
   * Output: FillableCell - the cell that was at the top of the stack before removal.
   * Pre-condition: None (handles empty stack gracefully via exception)
   * Post-condition: The top cell is removed from 'stack'; 'count' is incremented by 1.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public FillableCell pop()
  {
    if (getStack().isEmpty()) {
      throw new IndexOutOfBoundsException();
    }
    int lastIndex = getStack().size() - 1;
    FillableCell removed = getStack().remove(lastIndex);
    count++;
    return removed;
  }

    return count;

```java
   * Summary: Validates boundaries and cell eligibility, then assigns a pipe to a playable cell.
   * Algorithm: Check if row is in [1, rows-2] and col is in [1, cols-2]. Retrieve cell at cells[row][col]. Verify cell is an instance of FillableCell and cell.getPipe() == null. If all conditions pass, set cell.setPipe(pipe) and return true. Otherwise, return false.
   * Related Features: cell (2D array), rows, cols
   * Related Classes: FillableCell, Pipe
   * Input: row : int, col : int - internal array indices aligned with playable area (directly usable as array indices); pipe : Pipe - the pipe shape to place.
   * Output: boolean - true if placement succeeded, false if out of bounds, wrong cell type, or already occupied.
   * Pre-condition: None (bounds and type validation are performed internally).
   * Post-condition: Target cell is assigned the pipe if valid; otherwise, map state remains unchanged.

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
```

  public boolean tryPlacePipe(final int row, final int col, final Pipe pipe)
  {
    if (row >= 1 && row < getRows() - 1 && col >= 1 && col < getCols() - 1) {
        Cell c = getCell()[row][col];
        if (c instanceof FillableCell && ((FillableCell) c).getPipe() == null) {
            ((FillableCell) c).setPipe(pipe);
            return true;
        }
    }
    return false;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Propagates water across connected pipes up to a specified distance using Breadth-First Search (BFS).
   * Algorithm: Store current filledTiles as the starting frontier. Initialize newFilledCount = 0. Loop 'distance' times (each iteration represents one water propagation step): create nextFrontier list. For each tile in current frontier, get its connections via getConnections(). For each direction, calculate neighbor coordinate using offset. If neighbor is within bounds, is a FillableCell or TerminationCell, has a pipe/direction, and the neighbor's connection includes the opposite direction, mark neighbor as filled, add to nextFrontier, increment newFilledCount, and add to filledTiles list. Update current frontier = nextFrontier. After loop, set prevFilledTiles = newFilledCount.
   * Related Features: filledTiles, prevFilledTiles
   * Related Classes: Coordinate, Direction, Pipe, TerminationCell
   * Input: distance : int - maximum number of propagation steps this round.
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: At least one tile (e.g., sourceCell) is already marked as filled; distance >= 0.
   * Post-condition: All reachable pipes within 'distance' steps are marked filled and added to filledTiles; prevFilledTiles is updated with the count of tiles filled in this propagation round.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void fillTiles(final int distance)
  {
    EList<Coordinate> currentFrontier = new BasicEList<>(filledTiles);
    int newFilledCount = 0;
    
    for (int i = 0; i < distance; i++) {
        EList<Coordinate> nextFrontier = new BasicEList<>();
        
        for (Coordinate coord : currentFrontier) {
            Cell cellAtCoord = getCell()[coord.getRow()][coord.getCol()];
            Pipe pipe = cellAtCoord.getPipe();
            
            if (pipe != null) {
                for (Direction dir : pipe.getConnections()) {
                    int nRow = coord.getRow() + dir.getRowOffset();
                    int nCol = coord.getCol() + dir.getColOffset();
                    
                    if (nRow >= 1 && nRow < getRows() - 1 && nCol >= 1 && nCol < getCols() - 1) {
                        Cell neighbor = getCell()[nRow][nCol];
                        
                        if ((neighbor instanceof FillableCell || neighbor instanceof TerminationCell) && neighbor.getPipe() != null) {
                            Pipe neighborPipe = neighbor.getPipe();
                            
                            if (neighborPipe.getConnections().contains(dir.eOpposite())) {
                                if (neighbor instanceof FillableCell) {
                                    ((FillableCell) neighbor).setFilled(true);
                                }
                                
                                Coordinate newCoord = new Coordinate(nRow, nCol);
                                nextFrontier.add(newCoord);
                                newFilledCount++;
                                filledTiles.add(newCoord);
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        
        currentFrontier = nextFrontier;
    }
    
    setPrevFilledTiles(newFilledCount);
  }

Call map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe). 4. If successful, push the placed FillableCell to cellStack and increment numOfSteps by 1. 5. Return true on success, false on failure.

```java
   * Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack, Pipe

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

    }
    getPipeQueue().consume();
    FillableCell cell = getMap().tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe);
    if (cell != null) {
```

```java
   * Algorithm: 1. Convert column character to internal index: colIndex = col - 'A' + 1. 2. Retrieve the head pipe from pipeQueue (via peek()/consume()). 3. Call map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe). 4. If successful, push the placed FillableCell to cellStack and increment numOfSteps by 1. 5. Return true on success, false on failure.
   * Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
   * Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack, Pipe
   * Input: row : int - player-facing row index (directly maps to internal array row); col : char - player-facing column letter (e.g., 'A' maps to internal index 1).
   * Output: boolean - true if pipe placed successfully, false if placement failed or queue was empty.
   * Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty; row is in [1..rows-2] and col character maps to a valid column index in [1..cols-2].
   * Post-condition: Head pipe consumed from queue, placed on map if valid, corresponding FillableCell pushed to stack, numOfSteps incremented.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

    Pipe pipe = getPipeQueue().peek();
    if (pipe == null) {
        return false;
    }
    getPipeQueue().consume();
    FillableCell cell = getMap().tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe);
    if (cell != null) {
        getCellStack().push(cell);
        setNumOfSteps(getNumOfSteps() + 1);
        return true;
    }
    return false;
  }

Call pipeQueue.undo(cell.getPipe()) to restore the pipe to the queue head. 6. Increment numOfSteps by 1. 7. Return true.
   * Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
```

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * Summary: Places the head pipe from the queue onto the map at a player-specified coordinate.
   * Algorithm: 1. Convert column character to internal index: colIndex = col - 'A' + 1. 2. Retrieve the head pipe from pipeQueue (via peek()/consume()). 3. Call map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe). 4. If successful, push the placed FillableCell to cellStack and increment numOfSteps by 1. 5. Return true on success, false on failure.
   * Related Features: numOfSteps, cellStack, pipeQueue, map
   * Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack, Pipe
   * Input: row : int - player-facing row index (directly maps to internal array row); col : char - player-facing column letter (e.g., 'A' maps to internal index 1).
   * Output: boolean - true if pipe placed successfully, false if placement failed or queue was empty.
   * Pre-condition: pipeQueue is not empty; row is in [1..rows-2] and col character maps to a valid column index in [1..cols-2].
   * Post-condition: Head pipe consumed from queue, placed on map if valid, corresponding FillableCell pushed to stack, numOfSteps incremented.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean placePipe(final int row, final char col)
  {
    int colIndex = col - 'A' + 1;
    Pipe pipe = getPipeQueue().peek();
    if (pipe == null) {
        return false;
    }
    getPipeQueue().consume();
    if (getMap().tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipe)) {
        Cell placedCell = getMap().getCell()[row][colIndex];
        if (placedCell != null && placedCell instanceof FillableCell) {
            FillableCell cell = (FillableCell) placedCell;
            getCellStack().push(cell);
            setNumOfSteps(getNumOfSteps() + 1);
            return true;
        }
    }
    return false;
  }

   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
```java
   * Summary: Propagates water across connected pipes up to a specified distance using Breadth-First Search (BFS).
   * Algorithm: Store current filledTiles as the starting frontier. Initialize newFilledCount = 0. Loop 'distance' times (each iteration represents one water propagation step): create nextFrontier list. For each tile in current frontier, get its connections via getConnections(). For each direction, calculate neighbor coordinate using offset. If neighbor is within bounds, is a FillableCell or TerminationCell, has a pipe/direction, and the neighbor's connection includes the opposite direction, mark neighbor as filled, add to nextFrontier, increment newFilledCount, and add to filledTiles list. Update current frontier = nextFrontier. After loop, set prevFilledTiles = newFilledCount.
   * Related Features: filledTiles, prevFilledTiles
   * Related Classes: Coordinate, Direction, Pipe, TerminationCell
   * Input: distance : int - maximum number of propagation steps this round.
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: At least one tile (e.g., sourceCell) is already marked as filled; distance >= 0.
   * Post-condition: All reachable pipes within 'distance' steps are marked filled and added to filledTiles; prevFilledTiles is updated with the count of tiles filled in this propagation round.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void fillTiles(final int distance)
  {
    EList<Coordinate> currentFrontier = new BasicEList<>(filledTiles);
    int newFilledCount = 0;
    
    for (int i = 0; i < distance; i++) {
        EList<Coordinate> nextFrontier = new BasicEList<>();
        
        for (Coordinate coord : currentFrontier) {
            Cell cellAtCoord = getCell()[coord.getRow()][coord.getCol()];
            Pipe pipe = cellAtCoord.getPipe();
            
            if (pipe != null) {
                for (Direction dir : pipe.getConnections()) {
                    int nRow = coord.getRow() + dir.getRowOffset();
                    int nCol = coord.getCol() + dir.getColOffset();
                    
                    if (nRow >= 1 && nRow < getRows() - 1 && nCol >= 1 && nCol < getCols() - 1) {
                        Cell neighbor = getCell()[nRow][nCol];
                        
                        if ((neighbor instanceof FillableCell || neighbor instanceof TerminationCell) && neighbor.getPipe() != null) {

                            if (neighborPipe.getConnections().contains(dir.eOpposite())) {
                                if (neighbor instanceof FillableCell) {
                                    ((FillableCell) neighbor).setFilled(true);
                                }
                                
                                Coordinate newCoord = new Coordinate(nRow, nCol);
                                nextFrontier.add(newCoord);
                                newFilledCount++;
                                filledTiles.add(newCoord);
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        
        currentFrontier = nextFrontier;
    }
    
    setPrevFilledTiles(newFilledCount);
  }
```

```java
  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Clears a previously placed pipe from a specific map coordinate.
   * Algorithm: Retrieve cell at cells[coord.row][coord.col]. Set cell.setPipe(null). If coord exists in filledTiles, remove it from filledTiles.
   * Related Features: cell, filledTiles
   * Related Classes: Coordinate, FillableCell
   * Input: coord : Coordinate - the internal coordinates of the cell to clear.
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: The cell at the given coordinate contains a placed pipe.
   * Post-condition: Pipe reference is removed from the cell, reverting it to an empty state; coord is removed from filledTiles if present.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void undo(final Coordinate coord)
  {
    Cell c = getCell()[coord.getRow()][coord.getCol()];
    c.setPipe(null);
    if (filledTiles.contains(coord)) {
        filledTiles.remove(coord);
    }
  }

  public void fillBeginTile()
  { ... /* omitted */ }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Propagates water across connected pipes up to a specified distance using Breadth-First Search (BFS).
   * Algorithm: Store current filledTiles as the starting frontier. Initialize newFilledCount = 0. Loop 'distance' times (each iteration represents one water propagation step): create nextFrontier list. For each tile in current frontier, get its connections via getConnections(). For each direction, calculate neighbor coordinate using offset. If neighbor is within bounds, is a FillableCell or TerminationCell, has a pipe/direction, and the neighbor's connection includes the opposite direction, mark neighbor as filled, add to nextFrontier, increment newFilledCount, and add to filledTiles list. Update current frontier = nextFrontier. After loop, set prevFilledTiles = newFilledCount.
   * Related Features: filledTiles, prevFilledTiles
   * Related Classes: Coordinate, Direction, Pipe, TerminationCell
   * Input: distance : int - maximum number of propagation steps this round.
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: At least one tile (e.g., sourceCell) is already marked as filled; distance >= 0.
   * Post-condition: All reachable pipes within 'distance' steps are marked filled and added to filledTiles; prevFilledTiles is updated with the count of tiles filled in this propagation round.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void fillTiles(final int distance)
  {
    EList<Coordinate> currentFrontier = new BasicEList<>(filledTiles);
    int newFilledCount = 0;
    
    for (int i = 0; i < distance; i++) {
        EList<Coordinate> nextFrontier = new BasicEList<>();
        
        for (Coordinate coord : currentFrontier) {
            Cell cellAtCoord = getCell()[coord.getRow()][coord.getCol()];
            Pipe pipe = cellAtCoord.getPipe();
            
            if (pipe != null) {
                for (Direction dir : pipe.getConnections()) {
                    int nRow = coord.getRow() + dir.getRowOffset();
                    int nCol = coord.getCol() + dir.getColOffset();
                    
                    if (nRow >= 1 && nRow < getRows() - 1 && nCol >= 1 && nCol < getCols() - 1) {
                        Cell neighbor = getCell()[nRow][nCol];
                        
                        if ((neighbor instanceof FillableCell || neighbor instanceof TerminationCell) && neighbor.getPipe() != null) {
                            Pipe neighborPipe = neighbor.getPipe();
                            
                            if (neighborPipe.getConnections().contains(dir.eOpposite())) {
                                if (neighbor instanceof FillableCell) {
                                    ((FillableCell) neighbor).setFilled(true);
                                }
                                
                                Coordinate newCoord = new Coordinate(nRow, nCol);
                                nextFrontier.add(newCoord);
                                newFilledCount++;
                                filledTiles.add(newCoord);
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        
        currentFrontier = nextFrontier;
    }
    
    setPrevFilledTiles(newFilledCount);
  }
```

You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Propagates water across connected pipes up to a specified distance using Breadth-First Search (BFS).
   * Algorithm: Store current filledTiles as the starting frontier. Initialize newFilledCount = 0. Loop 'distance' times (each iteration represents one water propagation step): create nextFrontier list. For each tile in current frontier, get its connections via getConnections(). For each direction, calculate neighbor coordinate using offset. If neighbor is within bounds, is a FillableCell or TerminationCell, has a pipe/direction, and the neighbor's connection includes the opposite direction, mark neighbor as filled, add to nextFrontier, increment newFilledCount, and add to filledTiles list. Update current frontier = nextFrontier. After loop, set prevFilledTiles = newFilledCount.
   * Related Features: filledTiles, prevFilledTiles
   * Related Classes: Coordinate, Direction, Pipe, TerminationCell
   * Input: distance : int - maximum number of propagation steps this round.
   * Output: void
   * Pre-condition: At least one tile (e.g., sourceCell) is already marked as filled; distance >= 0.
   * Post-condition: All reachable pipes within 'distance' steps are marked filled and added to filledTiles; prevFilledTiles is updated with the count of tiles filled in this propagation round.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public void fillTiles(final int distance)
  {
    EList<Coordinate> currentFrontier = new BasicEList<>(filledTiles);
    int newFilledCount = 0;
    
    for (int i = 0; i < distance; i++) {
        EList<Coordinate> nextFrontier = new BasicEList<>();
        
        for (Coordinate coord : currentFrontier) {

                }
            }
```
